\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)/2\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)/2\relax}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Question} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Strong answer} \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Question} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Strong answer} \\
\endhead
Which plane owns the run's terminal outcome, and why can the LLM Gateway not own it even though every call passes through it? & Runtime and workflow. Position on the request path is not authority; the gateway sees model calls, not the run's state, acceptance criteria, or effects. \\*
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Why separate the global control tier from regional execution cells? & They have different scaling, availability, data-residency, and failure characteristics; immutable definitions can fan out while active tenant runs stay regional. Cells add scale additively and give tenant placement, residency, bounded blast radius, independent canaries, and predictable stateful-service limits. \\
Which trust boundary does indirect prompt injection cross, and which control there does not depend on detection? & Knowledge → model. ACL filtering before retrieval and instruction\slash{}data labeling (Chapter 6) hold even when no detector recognizes the attack. \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

\section{Where this leads next}

The reference architecture names a knowledge, context, and memory plane but not what fills it; Chapter 5 fills its knowledge half—the governed artifacts agents retrieve, reason over, are constrained by, and act through—and Chapter 6 its per-run context and memory.\par

\BookChapterCover{cover-images/chapter-05-give-knowledge-an-owner.png}

\BookChapter{05}{Give Knowledge an Owner}

\begin{bookcallout}[Where The Mortgage Case Stands]
Harborlight's evidence agent can retrieve the underwriting policy and quote it accurately. It still recommends proceeding with an application whose property or income profile the policy excludes, because retrieving a sentence is not the same as being bound by it. The platform team stops adding documents to the index and starts treating policy as governed knowledge. The second pay statement has arrived, and nothing yet tells a verified field from a model's reading of a PDF.
\end{bookcallout}

\begin{bookcallout}[The Design Choice]
Treat institutional knowledge as governed artifacts with owners, scope, and provenance, released like software and pinned per run — not as a corpus. Retrieval, traversal, and enforcement consume a release, never “current”.
\end{bookcallout}

\section{Why retrieval alone is not a knowledge strategy}

Chapter 4 gave knowledge, context, and memory one plane; this chapter fills its knowledge half. Teams often say “put the knowledge in a vector store” when they mean four jobs: supply context, support reasoning, enforce constraints, and enable action. Those jobs have different owners and failure modes, and the Harborlight case shows why one semantic index cannot be the evidence source, the inference model, the policy engine, and the tool catalog at once.\par

\section{Why similarity cannot tell you what to trust}

\BookDiagram{assets/diagrams/knowledge-fabric.pdf}{Figure 5-1 — The enterprise knowledge fabric and its four consumption modes}{knowledge-fabric}

\BookNeedspace{51.4pt}
\BookTableStart
\fontsize{7.2}{9}\selectfont
\begin{tabular}{@{}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)/5\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)/5\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)/5\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)/5\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)/5\relax}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries State owner & \color{white}\bfseries Trust boundary & \color{white}\bfseries Scale unit & \color{white}\bfseries Failure containment & \color{white}\bfseries Core contracts \\
Knowledge registry and Hub & authored source to released knowledge & artifact · domain · tenant & one release & KnowledgeArtifact · KnowledgeRelease · Rule · ResolutionQuery \\
\end{tabular}
\BookTableEnd

Retrieval-augmented generation answers one question well: \emph{what text is relevant to this?} Enterprises then ask it three questions it was never designed for; Figure 5-1 shows all four behind one governed resolution surface.\par

\BookNeedspace{126.9pt}
\BookTableStart
\fontsize{7.2}{9}\selectfont
\begin{tabular}{@{}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries Mode & \color{white}\bfseries The question & \color{white}\bfseries Representation & \color{white}\bfseries How it fails when wrong \\
Context & What is the situation? & Documents, embeddings, semantic search & Plausible grounding that is stale, superseded, or outside the reader's authority \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Reasoning & What should happen? & Ontology, knowledge graph (traversal), decision models (evaluation) & Correct facts, invalid inference \\
Constraints & What may I do? & Policies and permissions as enforceable rules & The agent explains the rule, then violates it \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Action & How do I do it? & Capability and API contracts & Right decision, wrong or unauthorized effect \\
\end{tabular}
\BookTableEnd

The fourth column is the argument: each mode fails differently and needs its own evidence and tests, and a similarity score is not evidence of authority, currency, or permission.\par

Harborlight hit the clearest case. Retrieval returns the policy that excludes a property or income condition, the model quotes it, and the agent proceeds anyway, because nothing was responsible for \emph{enforcing} the exclusion. The document was context. Nobody made it a constraint.\par

\section{Compliance knowledge is made of layers, and the layers are different kinds of thing}

Harborlight must enforce \textbf{compliance knowledge}, taken from regulations, standards, contracts, and internal policies. A \textbf{requirement} is a statement in an authoritative source that the organization must satisfy; an \textbf{obligation} is the duty derived from it, with subject, scope, modality, and effective period; a \textbf{control} discharges it (an enforced rule, check, approval, or review); a \textbf{constraint} is a control's true, false, or unknown reading at a control point; a \textbf{definition} is a term the others depend on. Chapters 13 and 21 bind obligations to one run or action.\par

Each layer derives from the one above and is a different kind of thing:\par

\BookNeedspace{153.1pt}
\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)/3\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)/3\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)/3\relax}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries Layer & \color{white}\bfseries Kind and identity & \color{white}\bfseries Checked by \\
Authoritative sources & Artifact: bytes, by digest & Digest match \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Extracted requirements and definitions & Artifact: candidate claims with span and verdict & \lstinline[style=bookinline,basicstyle=\ttfamily\color{BookNavyTwo}]!grounding! \\
Ontology & Model: kinds, relationships, properties, vocabulary; MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH & \lstinline[style=bookinline,basicstyle=\ttfamily\color{BookNavyTwo}]!knowledge-schema! \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Obligations, controls, definitions & Artifact: graph and rules in a release, by digest & Every gate and an approval \\
Approval and activation & Decisions, bound to a candidate digest & \lstinline[style=bookinline,basicstyle=\ttfamily\color{BookNavyTwo}]!independent-approval!, \lstinline[style=bookinline,basicstyle=\ttfamily\color{BookNavyTwo}]!release-integrity! \\
\end{tabular}
\BookTableEnd

The KnowledgeArtifact is the control-plane record; Chapter 6's KnowledgeObject is the Semantic view's projection of one inside a release. The reference implementation this chapter follows, the author's Policy Logic Forge repository \BookCitation{R48}, carries a source reference and evidence span on each rule field.\par

\section{How enterprise knowledge becomes usable}

Each representation below keeps its lineage to the layer above. A constraint that cannot point to the sentence or decision behind it is not an enforceable policy; it is an opinion encoded in software.\par

\subsection{Semantic knowledge}

Documents, policies, procedures, standards, and prior decisions, reached through embeddings and hybrid search. Chapter 6 owns how retrieved evidence enters a model call. What belongs here is what makes retrieval \emph{governed}: source authority, effective dates, jurisdiction, supersession, and the difference between text that appears in a document and text that is authoritative.\par

A retrieved passage should never reach a model without the metadata that decides whether it still applies.\par

\subsection{Ontology and knowledge graph}

Entities, relationships, dependencies, and the organization's own semantics. Traversal answers questions similarity cannot: \emph{what depends on this system three hops away?} No wording links a payments API to a regulatory filing obligation, so no embedding will connect them. The graph does.\par

\BookNeedspace{422.4pt}

\BookSnake · Traversal answering a question similarity cannot\par\nopagebreak

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={\BookSnake},language=Python]
@dataclass
class KnowledgeGraph:
    """A typed property graph: edges carry meaning, not just adjacency."""

    nodes: dict[str, dict] = field(default_factory=dict)
    edges: list[tuple[str, str, str]] = field(default_factory=list)

    def add(self, node_id: str, **properties) -> None:
        self.nodes[node_id] = properties

    def relate(self, source: str, relation: str, target: str) -> None:
        for end in (source, target):
            if end not in self.nodes:
                raise KeyError(f"unknown node: {end}")
        self.edges.append((source, relation, target))

    def neighbours(self, node_id: str, relation: str | None = None) -> list[str]:
        return [t for s, r, t in self.edges
                if s == node_id and (relation is None or r == relation)]

    def reaches(self, start: str, relation: str, *, max_hops: int = 6) -> list[str]:
        """Everything transitively reachable along one relation.

        Bounded: an unbounded traversal over an enterprise graph is a denial of
        service against your own agent, and a cycle makes it non-terminating.
        """
        seen, order = {start}, []
        queue = deque([(start, 0)])
        while queue:
            node, depth = queue.popleft()
            if depth >= max_hops:
                continue
            for nxt in self.neighbours(node, relation):
                if nxt not in seen:
                    seen.add(nxt)
                    order.append(nxt)
                    queue.append((nxt, depth + 1))
        return order

    def impact_of_changing(self, node_id: str) -> list[str]:
        """What depends on this, however indirectly. The question RAG cannot answer."""
        return self.reaches(node_id, "depends_on_inverse")
\end{lstlisting}

Traversal must be bounded. An unbounded walk over an enterprise graph is a denial of service against your own agent, and a cycle makes it non-terminating.\par

The \textbf{ontology} is the schema the graph conforms to: entity kinds, allowed relationships, required properties with types and units, and the SBVR vocabulary naming them. A statement is a typed node or edge, a typed rule, or a decision-table row; the graph format is the platform's choice. Conformance checks report every violation and repair none; a conforming graph freezes into an instance identified by its content digest.\par

\BookNeedspace{514.4pt}

\BookSnake · Traversal answering a question similarity cannot\par\nopagebreak

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={\BookSnake},language=Python]
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class GraphSchema:
    """The ontology: which kinds exist, how they may relate, what each must carry."""

    version: str                                  # MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH
    node_kinds: frozenset[str]
    edge_kinds: frozenset[tuple[str, str, str]]   # (source kind, relation, target kind)
    required: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]]          # kind -> properties it must carry


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Violation:
    subject: str
    reason: str


def validate(graph: KnowledgeGraph, schema: GraphSchema) -> list[Violation]:
    """Report every violation and repair nothing: a checker that fixes what it
    finds can no longer say how good the graph was."""
    found = []
    for node, props in sorted(graph.nodes.items()):
        kind = props.get("kind")
        if kind not in schema.node_kinds:
            found.append(Violation(node, f"kind {kind!r} is not in ontology {schema.version}"))
            continue
        found += [Violation(node, f"{kind} must carry {name}")
                  for name in schema.required.get(kind, ()) if name not in props]
    for source, relation, target in graph.edges:
        edge_kind = (graph.nodes[source].get("kind"), relation, graph.nodes[target].get("kind"))
        if edge_kind not in schema.edge_kinds:
            found.append(Violation(f"{source} {relation} {target}",
                                   f"ontology {schema.version} does not allow {edge_kind}"))
    return found


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class GraphRelease:
    """A conforming graph frozen for release: identity is the digest of its content."""

    ontology_version: str
    nodes: tuple
    edges: tuple
    digest: str

    @classmethod
    def freeze(cls, graph: KnowledgeGraph, schema: GraphSchema) -> "GraphRelease":
        if violations := validate(graph, schema):
            raise ValueError(f"{len(violations)} violations; a non-conforming graph is not released")
        nodes = tuple(sorted((n, tuple(sorted(p.items()))) for n, p in graph.nodes.items()))
        edges = tuple(sorted(graph.edges))
        content = repr((schema.version, nodes, edges)).encode("utf-8")
        return cls(schema.version, nodes, edges, hashlib.sha256(content).hexdigest())
\end{lstlisting}

Types come from declarations, never from names alone, as in the reference implementation \BookCitation{R48}. Reasoning means bounded traversal and deterministic evaluation, never open-ended inference, so a decision stays reproducible.\par

The honest caveat: ontologies are where enterprise knowledge programs go to die. Model only what an agent must traverse to decide, and let modeling justify itself one query at a time.\par

\subsection{Business rules as enforceable constraints}

The core of the layer. A rule an agent reads is documentation; a rule the platform evaluates at the effect boundary is a control. The move from prose to knowledge is not parsing — it is committing to a shape.\par

\BookNeedspace{505.2pt}

\BookSnake · A policy sentence as a typed, scoped rule with provenance\par\nopagebreak

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={\BookSnake},language=Python]
Modality = Literal["obligation", "permission", "prohibition"]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SourceRef:
    """Where a claim came from, precisely enough to check it again later."""

    document_id: str
    section_id: str
    start_offset: int
    end_offset: int
    source_sha256: str

    def excerpt(self, corpus: dict[str, str]) -> str:
        return corpus[self.document_id][self.start_offset:self.end_offset]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Predicate:
    variable: str
    operator: Literal["<", "<=", "=", "!=", ">=", ">"]
    value: Decimal | str | bool


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Rule:
    rule_id: str
    modality: Modality
    conditions: tuple[Predicate, ...]
    outcome: Predicate
    # Scope is required, not optional. A rule whose applicability is implicit
    # will be applied where it does not belong, which is the most common way a
    # correctly extracted rule still produces a wrong decision.
    jurisdiction: str
    responsible_party: str
    effective_from: str
    source: SourceRef
    supersedes: str | None = None
    exceptions: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)

    def applies_in(self, jurisdiction: str, on_date: str) -> bool:
        return jurisdiction == self.jurisdiction and on_date >= self.effective_from

    def digest(self) -> str:
        """Identity by content, so two extractions of one sentence agree."""
        payload = "|".join([
            self.modality, self.jurisdiction, self.responsible_party,
            *(f"{p.variable}{p.operator}{p.value}" for p in self.conditions),
            f"{self.outcome.variable}{self.outcome.operator}{self.outcome.value}",
        ])
        return hashlib.sha256(payload.encode()).hexdigest()[:16]
\end{lstlisting}

Scope is required: a rule whose applicability is implicit will be applied where it does not belong. Identity by content lets two independent extractions of one sentence recognize each other.\par

Enforcement then happens where intent becomes action:\par

\BookNeedspace{615.6pt}

\BookSnake · A policy rule enforced at the effect boundary\par\nopagebreak

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={\BookSnake},language=Python]
class PolicyViolation(Exception):
    """Refused by an enforceable rule. Carries the rule that refused it."""

    def __init__(self, message: str, rule_id: str, source_section: str) -> None:
        super().__init__(message)
        self.rule_id = rule_id
        self.source_section = source_section


class UnknownUnderPolicy(Exception):
    """A required input was missing, so the rule could not be evaluated.

    Refusing here is the whole point: a rule check that treats 'unknown' as
    'permitted' is not a control. The three-valued reading -- true, false,
    unknown -- is what keeps a missing field from silently authorizing an effect.
    """


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EnforcedRule:
    rule: object

    def _satisfied(self, facts: dict) -> bool:
        """Kleene AND: one false condition decides; otherwise a missing one is unknown."""
        missing = []
        for predicate in self.rule.conditions:
            if predicate.variable not in facts:
                missing.append(predicate.variable)
                continue
            value = facts[predicate.variable]
            checks = {">": value > predicate.value, ">=": value >= predicate.value,
                      "<": value < predicate.value, "<=": value <= predicate.value,
                      "=": value == predicate.value, "!=": value != predicate.value}
            if not checks[predicate.operator]:
                return False
        if missing:
            raise UnknownUnderPolicy(
                f"{self.rule.rule_id} needs {', '.join(missing)}, which was not supplied"
            )
        return True

    def enforce(self, facts: dict, *, jurisdiction: str, on_date: str) -> None:
        """Raise if this effect may not proceed. Silence means permitted."""
        if not self.rule.applies_in(jurisdiction, on_date):
            return  # out of scope: this rule has nothing to say here
        if not self._satisfied(facts):
            return  # conditions not met: the rule does not fire
        required = self.rule.outcome
        actual = facts.get(required.variable)
        if actual != required.value:
            raise PolicyViolation(
                f"{self.rule.rule_id} requires {required.variable}="
                f"{required.value}, got {actual!r}",
                rule_id=self.rule.rule_id,
                source_section=self.rule.source.section_id,
            )


def guarded_dispatch(effect, facts: dict, rules, *, jurisdiction: str, on_date: str):
    """Every enforced rule must pass before the effect is allowed to run."""
    for rule in rules:
        rule.enforce(facts, jurisdiction=jurisdiction, on_date=on_date)
    return effect()
\end{lstlisting}

Note the refusal on missing input. A rule check that reads \emph{unknown} as \emph{permitted} is not a control; three-valued evaluation stops an absent field from silently authorizing an effect.\par

\subsection{Business data catalog}

Entities, fields, definitions, ownership, lineage, sensitivity, and access constraints, described so an agent can discover and query data safely. A column name is not a definition, and a schema without a sensitivity class cannot tell an agent which fields it must not put in a prompt.\par

\subsection{Process and decision models}

Four OMG standards already describe what most enterprises otherwise invent badly. Ask of each what an agent can do with it that it could not do with prose.\par

\BookNeedspace{139.4pt}
\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)/3\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)/3\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)/3\relax}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries Standard & \color{white}\bfseries Represents & \color{white}\bfseries What an agent does with it \\
BPMN \BookCitation{R44} & Processes and their control flow & Plans against the real sequence, including the steps it may not skip \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
DMN \BookCitation{R45} & Decisions as tables with a hit policy & Evaluates a decision deterministically instead of reasoning about it \\
CMMN \BookCitation{R46} & Case management, unordered work & Handles work with no fixed sequence without inventing one \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
SBVR \BookCitation{R47} & Business vocabulary and rules & Resolves what a term means in this domain before acting on it \\
\end{tabular}
\BookTableEnd

DMN earns its place fastest: a table whose hit policy is proved over finite inputs turns judgment calls into a testable lookup. BPMN pays off where a sequence is genuinely fixed; CMMN and SBVR where case work or vocabulary ambiguity is the problem. The reference implementation \BookCitation{R48} emits DMN and BPMN as fail-closed projections, BPMN only where the source states an order.\par

\section{Turn policy prose into logic the system can enforce}

Extraction is probabilistic. Verification must not be. The pipeline that works separates the two and refuses to let the first vouch for itself.\par

Consider a policy sentence that accepts a pay statement only within an age window. The runtime cannot safely enforce prose. The platform extracts a candidate rule with its exact source span, checks that the span says what the rule says, has an eligible owner approve the interpretation, compiles it, and records the version used for each case. Skip a link and the system may enforce a rule nobody can explain or prove is current.\par

\BookDiagram{assets/diagrams/policy-to-guardrail.pdf}{Figure 5-2 — Extract, prove, approve, and project: from policy text to an enforced constraint}{policy-to-guardrail}

\textbf{Extract} is the part a language model does well: find the rules, type their conditions and outcomes, record where each came from.\par

\textbf{Prove} is the part that decides whether to believe it. Verification does not trust the citation a rule carries; it re-opens the pinned source, admits the claim only if its quoted text is literally present in the cited span, and records the evidence sliced from those bytes. A literal span proves the quote is real, not that it supports the rule: the reference verifier \BookCitation{R48} lets a model judge support only over evidence resolved as literal corpus text, and the listing keeps that deterministic half.\par

\BookNeedspace{523.6pt}

\BookSnake · Admitting a claim only on a literal span of the bytes it cites\par\nopagebreak

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={\BookSnake},language=Python]
Status = Literal["supported", "contradicted", "insufficient_evidence"]
HELD: Status = "insufficient_evidence"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Verdict:
    status: Status
    reason: str
    evidence: str | None = None  # sliced from the pinned bytes, never from the claim

    @property
    def grounded(self) -> bool:
        return self.status == "supported"


def _folded(text: str) -> tuple[str, list[int]]:
    """Case- and whitespace-folded text, with each character's source offset."""
    chars: list[str] = []
    offsets: list[int] = []
    for i, ch in enumerate(text):
        if ch.isspace() and (not chars or chars[-1] == " "):
            continue
        chars.append(" " if ch.isspace() else ch.lower())
        offsets.append(i)
    return "".join(chars).rstrip(), offsets


def verify(quote: str, source_ref, corpus: dict[str, str]) -> Verdict:
    """Admit a claim only on a literal span of the bytes it cites.

    Checked in order, because a later check cannot be trusted once an earlier
    one has failed.
    """
    document = corpus.get(source_ref.document_id)
    if document is None:
        return Verdict(HELD, "cited document does not exist")
    # The digest pins the document *version*. Without it, a rule extracted from
    # last year's policy still 'verifies' against this year's text.
    actual = hashlib.sha256(document.encode()).hexdigest()
    if source_ref.source_sha256 not in {actual, TEST_SENTINEL}:
        return Verdict(HELD, "cited document has changed since extraction")
    if not 0 <= source_ref.start_offset < source_ref.end_offset <= len(document):
        return Verdict(HELD, "cited span falls outside the document")
    span = document[source_ref.start_offset:source_ref.end_offset]
    span_folded, offsets = _folded(span)
    quote_folded, _ = _folded(quote.strip())
    # Literal presence, not term overlap: a paraphrase that shares nine words in
    # ten is still the model's sentence, not the source's.
    at = span_folded.find(quote_folded) if quote_folded else -1
    if at < 0:
        return Verdict(HELD, "claim is not supported by the cited passage")
    evidence = span[offsets[at]:offsets[at + len(quote_folded) - 1] + 1]
    return Verdict("supported", "quoted text found in the cited span", evidence)
\end{lstlisting}

A model that invents a rule will invent a plausible citation, so a well-formed citation proves nothing; what matters is whether the cited passage \emph{says what the rule says}. In one run of the author's own compliance extraction pipeline built on this design, independent verification rejected 44\% of the model-proposed dependency and conflict claims that a single structural screen had already accepted \BookCitation{R48}. That figure is author-reported, one measurement on one corpus, not a general rate.\par

\textbf{Approve.} An eligible owner accepts the interpretation; the approval, the source digest, and the rule version are recorded together. Verification shows the rule matches the text; approval makes it the organization's rule, bound to the candidate digest of the whole change (§ Change compliance knowledge the way you change software).\par

\textbf{Project} turns what survived into the standards-compliant models above, and into the constraints the runtime enforces at the effect boundary.\par

The age-window rule now needs a fact, the statement's age, and that fact comes from a document too.\par

\begin{bookantipattern}[Common pitfall]
Treating extraction confidence as grounding. A high-confidence rule with a fabricated citation is more dangerous than a low-confidence one, because it will pass review. Verify against the corpus, not against the model's own certainty.
\end{bookantipattern}

\section{Ingest documents the way you extract rules}

An applicant's pay statement arrives through a channel the platform does not trust. Until it is verified it is not evidence; it is bytes.\par

\textbf{Intake.} Intake computes the digest, inspects the content, classifies the kind, records origin, tenant, case, ACL pointer, and retention class, and labels the artifact as data before any model sees it (Chapter 20's ingest row). The digest is authoritative; the text, like every index entry, is a projection carrying its source revision, ACL pointer, and deletion state. A statement resubmitted under a new name has the first one's digest, so it is not a second statement.\par

\textbf{Extract and normalize.} A model proposes typed fields, each with its region, a confidence, and the extractor version: model route and prompt digest. Normalization puts the unit on the field, parses dates and money, and marks each value \emph{extracted} (the document states it) or \emph{derived} (the platform computed it, such as the statement's age in days). The document kind's schema, not the model, declares which fields are decision-material, and a field the schema does not declare is held, never guessed into place.\par

\BookNeedspace{450.0pt}

\BookSnake · A document's fields verified against its bytes before they become facts\par\nopagebreak

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={\BookSnake},language=Python]
Route = Literal["human_review", "targeted_repair", "case_management"]

# Which fields can block is the platform's declaration, never the extractor's.
PAY_STATEMENT = {"employer": True, "pay_date": True, "gross_pay": True,
                 "deductions": True, "net_pay": True, "ytd_gross": True,
                 "pay_statement_age_days": True, "employer_address": False}


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class DocumentRecord:
    """Intake's record of the bytes: the digest is authoritative, text a projection."""

    document_id: str
    kind: str
    sha256: str
    source_revision: str
    case_id: str
    acl_ref: str
    extractor_version: str  # model route and prompt digest
    label: str = "data"     # the text is data, never instruction


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ExtractedField:
    name: str
    value: Decimal | str | int
    unit: str | None              # the unit lives on the field: "USD", "days"
    origin: Literal["extracted", "derived"]
    region: SourceRef | None = None   # extracted: where the document states it
    quote: str = ""                   # extracted: the text the model says it read
    inputs: tuple[str, ...] = ()      # derived: the fields it is computed from
    confidence: float = 0.0           # recorded for evaluation, never used to decide


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class FieldVerdict:
    name: str
    status: Status
    reason: str
    route: Route | None = None


def duplicate_of(record: DocumentRecord, seen: dict[str, str]) -> str | None:
    """Same bytes under a new name are the earlier document, not a second one."""
    return seen.get(record.sha256)
\end{lstlisting}

The gate in Figure 5-3 is Figure 5-2's: verification reads the bytes, not the extraction.\par

\BookDiagram{assets/diagrams/document-ingestion.pdf}{Figure 5-3 — Document ingestion: intake, extract, verify against the bytes, route, record, serve}{document-ingestion}

\textbf{Verify.} Verification re-opens the pinned bytes and checks, in order: the digest matches; each extracted field's quoted text is literally in its region, through the rule lane's verifier; the value parsed from those bytes equals the record's; the record is internally consistent (net equals gross minus deductions); and it agrees with the case, such as the employer on the application. Derived fields are checked against their verified inputs, never against text; no statement prints its own age. Each field receives exactly one verdict (supported, contradicted, or insufficient evidence); a verifier that skips one fails closed. Admission then follows the grounding gate (§ Change compliance knowledge the way you change software): no facts until every decision-material field is supported. Confidence plays no part.\par

\BookNeedspace{523.6pt}

\BookSnake · A document's fields verified against its bytes before they become facts\par\nopagebreak

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={\BookSnake},language=Python]
def verify_document(record, fields, text, *, case, derive, verifier=ground):
    if hashlib.sha256(text.encode()).hexdigest() != record.sha256:
        held = {f.name: FieldVerdict(f.name, HELD, "bytes changed since extraction",
                                     "case_management") for f in fields}
        return Certification(record, held, complete=True, route="case_management")
    out: dict[str, FieldVerdict] = {}
    values: dict[str, object] = {}

    def fail(names, status, why, route):
        for n in names:
            out[n] = FieldVerdict(n, status, why, route)

    for f in (f for f in fields if f.origin == "extracted"):
        v = verifier(f.quote, f.region, {record.document_id: text})
        if v is None:
            continue  # no verdict for this field: the run is incomplete
        if not v.grounded:
            fail([f.name], v.status, v.reason, "targeted_repair")
        elif _parse(v.evidence, f.value) != f.value:  # the bytes decide, not the quote
            fail([f.name], "contradicted", f"bytes say {v.evidence!r}", "human_review")
        else:
            values[f.name] = f.value
    agree = [((k,), lambda g, k=k, e=e: g[k] == e, f"{k} disagrees with the case")
             for k, e in case.items()]
    for names, holds, why in (*CONSISTENCY, *agree):
        if all(n in values for n in names) and not holds(values):
            fail(names, "contradicted", why, "human_review")  # cannot tell which lies
    for f in (f for f in fields if f.origin == "derived"):
        if not all(n in values and n not in out for n in f.inputs):
            fail([f.name], HELD, "an input is not supported", "case_management")
        elif derive[f.name](values) != f.value:  # checked structurally, never as text
            fail([f.name], "contradicted", "derivation disagrees", "human_review")
        else:
            values[f.name] = f.value
    for f in fields:
        if f.name not in PAY_STATEMENT:
            fail([f.name], HELD, "not a declared field of this kind", None)
        elif f.name in values and f.name not in out:
            out[f.name] = FieldVerdict(f.name, "supported", "verified against bytes")
    named = {f.name for f in fields}
    fail([n for n, m in PAY_STATEMENT.items() if m and n not in named],
         HELD, "nothing extracted", "targeted_repair")
    complete = all(f.name in out for f in fields)  # one verdict per field
    blocking = [v for v in out.values()
                if PAY_STATEMENT.get(v.name) and v.status != "supported"]
    route = next((r for r in PRIORITY if any(v.route == r for v in blocking)), None)
    facts = {n: values[n] for n in values if out[n].status == "supported"}
    return Certification(record, out, facts, complete, complete and not blocking, route)


def facts_for_rules(cert: Certification) -> dict[str, object]:
    """Only a servable document yields facts; anything else leaves the rule unknown."""
    return dict(cert.values) if cert.servable else {}
\end{lstlisting}

\begin{booksecurity}[Security and governance]
Verification proves the document says it, not that it is true. An internally consistent forgery passes this gate; only an independent source, such as the payroll provider of Chapter 17 or a human reviewer, catches it. Record corroboration separately, and never let a decision that needs the truth rest on verification alone.
\end{booksecurity}

\textbf{Route and record.} Findings route by kind: a contradiction to human review (an InterventionTask, Chapter 14), an unlocated field to targeted repair of that field alone through the same gate, a missing input or unhandled kind to case management. A reviewer's correction is a new version, and the model's value stays in history; a job that extracted nothing reports a finding, never success. Everything lands in one record, the \textbf{KnowledgeArtifact} named in the chapter's first table: kind (document, chunk, field, rule), source digest and region, extractor and verifier versions, per-field verdicts, corroboration, status and route, owner, version and supersedes, effective range, scope, ACL, and data class. Status follows the knowledge lifecycle defined below, Draft through Revoked; \emph{retired} is not a status but the retention event that follows deprecation or revocation. Deleting the source deletes the record, its history, and its derived facts through the lineage events Chapter 6 lists.\par

\textbf{Version and re-process.} When the bytes, the extractor (Chapter 1's Friday), or the consuming policy changes, re-processing is a diff, not an overwrite: every field is changed, unchanged, review-held, or refused, nothing is dropped, only changed facts trigger re-evaluation, and a field under review never resolves itself. A run pins the knowledge release it read, whose manifest lists every policy record's digest, and its ContextManifests name the case documents' record versions, so a six-month-old decision can be reproduced with its evidence.\par

At the effect boundary the age-window rule reads \lstinline[style=bookinline]!pay_statement_age_days! only from a servable record; otherwise its missing-input refusal fires. A forged net goes to review, so the rule still sees \emph{unknown}; altered bytes fail the digest check; and the Tuesday instruction in an upload is labelled text, not a declared field, so it directs neither rule nor model.\par

\section{Why provenance determines what you can trust}

Every representation above is only as good as the metadata that says whether to believe it.\par

\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)/2\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)/2\relax}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries Field & \color{white}\bfseries Why an agent needs it \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries Field & \color{white}\bfseries Why an agent needs it \\
\endhead
Owner & Who answers when the artifact is wrong \\*
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Version and supersedes & Which of two conflicting statements is current \\*
Effective from \slash{} until & Whether it applied on the date in question \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Jurisdiction and scope & Whether it applies to this subject at all \\
Provenance & The passage, offsets, and document digest it derives from: content provenance, distinct from the build provenance of released artifacts in Chapter 27 \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Confidence & Authoritative or inferred \\
Verification and corroboration & Whether the source says it, and whether an independent source agrees \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Permissions and data class & Who may see it, and whether it may enter a prompt \\
Release and status & Which release carries this version, in which lifecycle state: what a run pins \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Lifecycle & Review cadence and expiry \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

Without the document digest, a rule extracted from last year's policy still verifies against this year's text, and nothing notices that the ground moved.\par

\begin{booksecurity}[Security and governance]
Permissions belong to the knowledge artifact, enforced at the resolution boundary. Filtering retrieved passages after the model has seen them is not access control; it is redaction after disclosure.
\end{booksecurity}

\section{Two planes: develop compliance knowledge, consume releases}

Compliance knowledge is developed in one plane and used in another (Figure 5-4). The \textbf{control plane}, in Chapter 4's global control tier, extracts, validates, versions, approves, publishes, and deprecates; owners and SMEs write to it, an assistant only proposes, and one service identity, the \textbf{registry publisher}, releases and activates. The reference implementation \BookCitation{R48} is a control plane with no runtime consumer.\par

What the platform adds beyond it starts at the interface. The \textbf{knowledge registry} holds ontology versions, objects, release manifests, and a live pointer per stream, tenant, environment, and region. A \textbf{knowledge release} is the immutable, content-addressed set of objects, ontology version, and source revisions the publisher activated. At admission each stream the ReleaseManifest declares resolves to a release digest in the RunManifest's \lstinline[style=bookinline]!knowledge_snapshots! (Chapter 4). The \textbf{runtime plane} then reads only that pin, through the Hub's resolve (admission only), search, bounded query, and three-valued evaluate operations, none of which accepts “current”.\par

\BookDiagram{assets/diagrams/knowledge-lifecycle.pdf}{Figure 5-4 — Compliance knowledge has two planes: the control plane develops and releases it, the runtime plane enforces one pinned release per run, and only evidence crosses back up}{knowledge-lifecycle}

Releases cross down; evidence crosses up as decision records, refusals, contradictions, SME corrections, and Chapter 25's ChangeBundle drafts, never as a write. No control point re-resolves a stream mid-run; stale knowledge needs a new run or a migration (Chapter 15).\par

\section{Change compliance knowledge the way you change software}

A \textbf{knowledge change} is the reviewable unit. It names its objects by stable id, its rationale and source digests, and its version bump, and resolves into one candidate whose \textbf{candidate digest} covers the content, the pinned upstream base, the live release it replaces, and the resolver version. A change deprecates, never deletes; removing or renaming an ontology kind is a MAJOR version with a migration. Chapter 27 § Knowledge releases covers the CI/CD stages, tenant extensions, promotion, and rollback.\par

Gates run on the resolved candidate, not the textual diff. A model may locate evidence or propose a merge, a verdict, or a change; only a deterministic check turns it into a gate result. The reference implementation \BookCitation{R48} runs structural invariants, then targeted repair of only the failed fields, then independent grounding rebuilt from the corpus. The gates keep that order and add what a release needs that a build does not.\par

\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)/3\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)/3\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)/3\relax}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries Gate & \color{white}\bfseries Checks & \color{white}\bfseries Fails closed on \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries Gate & \color{white}\bfseries Checks & \color{white}\bfseries Fails closed on \\
\endhead
\lstinline[style=bookinline,basicstyle=\ttfamily\color{BookNavyTwo}]!knowledge-schema! & Kinds, relationships, properties, units; the rule contract & A blocking violation \\*
\rowcolor{BookLight}
\lstinline[style=bookinline,basicstyle=\ttfamily\color{BookNavyTwo}]!change-contract! & Touched equals declared; rationale, sources, version & A mismatch \\*
\lstinline[style=bookinline,basicstyle=\ttfamily\color{BookNavyTwo}]!duplicate-overlap! & Identical objects merge, keeping all provenance & Lost provenance \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
\lstinline[style=bookinline,basicstyle=\ttfamily\color{BookNavyTwo}]!grounding! & A literal span in the pinned bytes; one verdict per claim & An unsupported decision-material claim; incomplete output \\
\lstinline[style=bookinline,basicstyle=\ttfamily\color{BookNavyTwo}]!consistency! & Same subject and scope, incompatible outcomes; finite domains enumerated; undefined terms held & An unresolved candidate; an unproved table \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
\lstinline[style=bookinline,basicstyle=\ttfamily\color{BookNavyTwo}]!regression! & Golden and boundary cases; every object changed, unchanged, review-held, or refused & A regressed case; an object without status \\
\lstinline[style=bookinline,basicstyle=\ttfamily\color{BookNavyTwo}]!staleness! & Revisions, base, digests, and input fingerprints still match & Any mismatch: Stale \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
\lstinline[style=bookinline,basicstyle=\ttfamily\color{BookNavyTwo}]!independent-approval! & An eligible SME, not the author, approved this digest & Self-approval; another digest \\
\lstinline[style=bookinline,basicstyle=\ttfamily\color{BookNavyTwo}]!release-integrity! & Bundle verified; version once; pointer compare-and-swap; every object accounted for & Any failure: the old release stays live \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

Gates prove form, provenance, and bounded properties; consistency is a filter for the SME, not a proof, and only the approver judges meaning. A finding holds an object as \lstinline[style=bookinline]!review-held!, excluded from evaluation but visible in the release, and routes it by kind: a contradiction or judgment call to human review, a structure defect to targeted repair that re-enters the gate, an evidence gap to case management, as for documents. Every gate reports objects examined over objects in the release; an empty suite reports zero, not a pass.\par

\BookNeedspace{339.6pt}

\BookSnake · Gate findings routed by kind, and a quality budget that cannot pass by rounding\par\nopagebreak

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={\BookSnake},language=Python]
Route = Literal["human_review", "targeted_repair", "case_management"]

REPAIRABLE = {"contract", "structure", "naming", "test_case"}   # deterministic defects
EVIDENCE_GAPS = {"evidence_gap", "unhandled_kind"}


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Finding:
    """A gate's finding holds an object; it never removes it."""

    object_id: str
    kind: str
    reason: str
    human_required: bool = False   # an explicit judgment finding


def route(findings: Iterable[Finding]) -> Route | None:
    """Only a contradiction or an explicit judgment call needs a person's time.

    A structure defect is repaired narrowly and re-enters the same gate; a gap in
    the evidence is worked as a case; a kind nobody planned for fails closed.
    """
    found = list(findings)
    if not found:
        return None
    if any(f.kind == "contradiction" or f.human_required for f in found):
        return "human_review"
    kinds = {f.kind for f in found}
    if kinds <= REPAIRABLE:
        return "targeted_repair"
    if kinds <= REPAIRABLE | EVIDENCE_GAPS:
        return "case_management"
    return "human_review"
\end{lstlisting}

Consistency rests on operations over typed rule sets. A union keeps each rule's sources and identifiers; a contradiction is surfaced with its evidence and never settled by picking a winner; model-judged equivalence never overrides an alignment by identifier or citation. The reference implementation \BookCitation{R48} adds finite-domain proofs this listing omits.\par

\BookNeedspace{109.6pt}

\BookSnake · Union, intersection, difference and contradiction over rule sets\par\nopagebreak

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={\BookSnake},language=Python]
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Attributed:
    """A rule together with every source that asserts it."""

    rule: object
    sources: tuple[str, ...]
    original_ids: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Contradiction:
    """Both sides preserved. Resolution is a human decision, not a merge rule."""

    subject: str
    left: object
    right: object
    left_source: str
    right_source: str
    reason: str


def _key(rule) -> str:
    """Rules align by content, so independent extractions of one policy match."""
    return rule.digest()


def _subject(rule) -> tuple[str, str, str]:
    """What a rule is *about*: the same subject is what makes a clash possible."""
    return (rule.outcome.variable, rule.jurisdiction,
            tuple(sorted((p.variable, p.operator, p.value) for p in rule.conditions)))


def union(left: dict[str, object], right: dict[str, object]) -> list[Attributed]:
    """Every distinct rule, each carrying the sources that assert it."""
    names = list(left) + [n for n in right if n not in left]
    by_key: dict[str, Attributed] = {}
    for source in names:
        for rule in (left.get(source) or right.get(source)):
            key = _key(rule)
            existing = by_key.get(key)
            if existing is None:
                by_key[key] = Attributed(rule, (source,), {source: rule.rule_id})
            else:
                by_key[key] = Attributed(
                    existing.rule, existing.sources + (source,),
                    {**existing.original_ids, source: rule.rule_id},
                )
    return list(by_key.values())


def intersection(left: dict[str, object], right: dict[str, object]) -> list[Attributed]:
    """Rules both sources assert -- corroborated, not yet verified or authorized."""
    both = {s for s in left} | {s for s in right}
    return [a for a in union(left, right) if len(set(a.sources)) == len(both)]


def difference(left: dict[str, object], right: dict[str, object]) -> list[Attributed]:
    """Rules only the left source asserts -- scope-dependent, not yet safe."""
    right_keys = {_key(r) for rules in right.values() for r in rules}
    name = next(iter(left))
    return [Attributed(r, (name,), {name: r.rule_id})
            for r in left[name] if _key(r) not in right_keys]


def contradictions(left: dict[str, object], right: dict[str, object]) -> list[Contradiction]:
    """Rule pairs about the same subject that cannot both be satisfied."""
    left_name, right_name = next(iter(left)), next(iter(right))
    found = []
    for a in left[left_name]:
        for b in right[right_name]:
            if _subject(a) != _subject(b):
                continue
            if a.outcome.value != b.outcome.value or a.modality != b.modality:
                found.append(Contradiction(
                    subject=a.outcome.variable, left=a, right=b,
                    left_source=left_name, right_source=right_name,
                    reason=(f"{left_name} requires {a.outcome.variable}="
                            f"{a.outcome.value} ({a.modality}); {right_name} requires "
                            f"{b.outcome.value} ({b.modality}) under the same conditions"),
                ))
    return found
\end{lstlisting}

\subsection{What the platform adds beyond the reference implementation}

The reference implementation stops at a certified build. Release states reuse Chapter 8's names, add Approved because meaning has no automated oracle, and drop Staged because a release is Active per environment:\par

\BookNeedspace{154.3pt}
\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)/2\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)/2\relax}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries State & \color{white}\bfseries Means \\
Draft & Not resolvable; flagged Stale if its target moved \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Certified & Complete gate record; held objects excluded from evaluation \\
Approved & Independently approved on this digest; any edit returns it to Draft \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Active & Named by a live pointer; new runs resolve it \\
Deprecated & Kept for runs that pinned it, through the compatibility window \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Revoked & Not resolvable; in-flight runs pause or migrate by risk class \\
\end{tabular}
\BookTableEnd

Retirement follows as a retention event; a release is kept while any retained RunManifest references it. \textbf{Activation is the publication boundary}: the publisher rechecks, stores and verifies the bundle, then moves the pointer. Until then the runtime cannot see the candidate, and a failed publish leaves the old release live. Activation changes behavior without an agent release, so it is a Chapter 26 change class with independent approval as the floor.\par

The analogy breaks where meaning matters: a clean merge is not valid knowledge, sources change upstream on their own schedule, and two jurisdictions may legitimately disagree.\par

\section{Obligations enforced at every control point}

Chapter 1's control points, the plan compiler, and the acceptance engine read the same pinned release; the latter two remain lifecycle controls, not control points. Knowledge is \textbf{approved} for a run when the publisher activated it after a complete gate record and an independent approval, the RunManifest pinned it, and it is not Revoked. Every decision records the release digest.\par

\BookNeedspace{97.3pt}
\BookTableStart
\fontsize{7.2}{9}\selectfont
\begin{tabular}{@{}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries Consumer & \color{white}\bfseries Reads & \color{white}\bfseries Missing, ambiguous, or revoked & \color{white}\bfseries Contradicted \\
Context Service (Chapter 6) & Semantic view & Omit; held text is never a fact & Serve both, attributed \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Plan compiler (Chapter 12) & Bound rules and tables & Refuse the node & Refuse and escalate \\
Policy decision point (Chapter 21) & Catalog definitions & Deny protected actions & Deny \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Tool Gateway (Chapter 22) & Enforced rules, verified facts & Refuse; missing input is \lstinline[style=bookinline,basicstyle=\ttfamily\color{BookNavyTwo}]!UnknownUnderPolicy! & Refuse \\
Acceptance engine (Chapter 13) & Domain rules, criteria & Reject: evidence missing & Escalate \\
\end{tabular}
\BookTableEnd

Three consultation rules come straight from the reference evaluator \BookCitation{R48}: only a completely lowered rule is evaluable, and an unproved table is refused before evaluation; unknown refuses, never defaults; incompatible assignments to one subject are refused, never adjudicated. The fourth is the platform's: that evaluator returns unknown for any rule scoped by jurisdiction or date, having no runtime context, and the control point supplies it.\par

\BookNeedspace{422.4pt}

\BookSnake · A policy rule enforced at the effect boundary\par\nopagebreak

\begin{lstlisting}[style=bookcode,title={\BookSnake},language=Python]
ReleaseStatus = Literal["draft", "certified", "approved", "active", "deprecated", "revoked"]


class ReleaseNotEvaluable(Exception):
    """The run's pin names a release no control point may evaluate."""


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PinnedRelease:
    """One knowledge release as a run pinned it at admission (RunManifest)."""

    stream: str
    digest: str
    status: ReleaseStatus
    rules: tuple[EnforcedRule, ...]


def rules_for(pins: dict[str, PinnedRelease], stream: str) -> tuple[EnforcedRule, ...]:
    """The rules this run may evaluate: from its own pin, never from 'current'.

    Only an activated release is evaluable. Deprecated still serves the run that
    pinned it; revoked serves nobody; anything not yet active was never released.
    """
    pin = pins.get(stream)
    if pin is None:
        raise UnknownUnderPolicy(f"the run pinned no release of {stream}")
    if pin.status not in ("active", "deprecated"):
        raise ReleaseNotEvaluable(f"{stream}@{pin.digest[:12]} is {pin.status}")
    return pin.rules


def pinned_dispatch(effect, facts: dict, pins: dict[str, PinnedRelease], stream: str,
                    *, jurisdiction: str, on_date: str):
    """Enforce the pinned release; every outcome cites the digest it was decided on."""
    rules = rules_for(pins, stream)
    try:
        result = guarded_dispatch(effect, facts, rules,
                                  jurisdiction=jurisdiction, on_date=on_date)
    except PolicyViolation as refusal:
        refusal.release = pins[stream].digest
        raise
    return result, pins[stream].digest
\end{lstlisting}

When Harborlight's upstream requirement on current income evidence shortens the age window, consistency reads the two thresholds as declared supersession, and an income rule whose term “qualifying income” is undefined is held. The rest is the platform's: the underwriting policy owner, not the author, approves; \lstinline[style=bookinline]!mortgage-policy@2026.10! activates in staging and is promoted by digest; \lstinline[style=bookinline]!RUN-98217! keeps \lstinline[style=bookinline]!2026.09!; a plan node needing the held rule is refused at compile time. When a wrong value is found, production rolls back by pointer and runs on \lstinline[style=bookinline]!2026.10! are listed for re-evaluation.\par

\section{Reference architecture: the Enterprise Knowledge Hub}

Agents must not hold connection strings to six stores. They ask one service, which resolves, authorizes, and attributes.\par

\BookNeedspace{170.3pt}
\BookTableStart
\fontsize{7.2}{9}\selectfont
\begin{tabular}{@{}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries Hub view & \color{white}\bfseries Holds & \color{white}\bfseries Answers & \color{white}\bfseries Serves mode \slash{} built from \\
Governance (registry) & Ownership, versions, releases, effective dates, permissions & May this run see this, and which release is it in? & All modes \slash{} § Two planes: develop compliance knowledge, consume releases \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Semantic & Documents and embeddings & What text is relevant? & Context \slash{} § Semantic knowledge \\
Structural & Ontology, graph, data catalog & What is related to what, and what does this field mean? & Reasoning \slash{} § Ontology and knowledge graph, § Business data catalog \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Normative & Rules, decision tables, vocabulary & What is required, permitted, or forbidden? & Constraints \slash{} § Business rules as enforceable constraints, DMN, SBVR \\
Procedural & Process and case models & In what order, and what may not be skipped? & Reasoning \slash{} BPMN, CMMN \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Capability & Tools, APIs, effects, prerequisites & What can be done, and at what cost? & Action \slash{} the capability catalog, Chapter 8 \\
\end{tabular}
\BookTableEnd

Ingestion fills the Semantic view with labelled text and the Normative view's rules with supported facts. The Hub is the knowledge half of Chapter 4's knowledge, context, and memory plane, with two callers: the Context Service before a step (Chapter 6) and read-only tools through the Tool Gateway during one (Chapter 3). Both resolve against the run's pinned release with the same permissions and attribution; neither holds store connections.\par

\section{A sensible place to start}

The examples above are the reference implementation, and each is executed in continuous integration. Composed, they show the Hub's shape: a rule extracted with provenance, verified, placed in a conforming graph, reconciled against a second source, released through gates and an independent approval, and enforced from a run's pin over verified document facts.\par

\section{If this fails in production}

\BookNeedspace{255.9pt}

\color{white}\bfseries Symptom & \color{white}\bfseries Likely cause & \color{white}\bfseries First check \\

\rowcolor{BookLight}

\rowcolor{BookLight}

If the vector index is being treated as the source of truth, the repair is structural; see AP-09 in Chapter 17.\par

\section{What to verify before production}

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Every knowledge artifact has an owner, a version, an effective range, and a provenance reference that resolves.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Retrieval results carry authority and currency metadata, not just a similarity score.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} At least one rule is enforced at the effect boundary, with a test proving the effect does not run when it refuses.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Missing inputs refuse rather than default to permitted.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Every extracted field resolves to a region in a digest-pinned document and carries a verdict; every check has a consumer that can refuse.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Union preserves per-source attribution; contradictions are escalated, never auto-resolved.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Every RunManifest names the knowledge release digests it resolved; no control point reads “current”.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} No knowledge change reaches a live pointer without a complete gate record and an independent approval of its candidate digest; rollback rewrites nothing.\par

\textbf{\ensuremath{\checkmark}} Permissions are enforced at the resolution boundary, not after the model has seen the passage.\par

\section{Put the idea to work}

\begin{enumerate}

\item Take one policy sentence from your own organization and express it as a typed rule with scope and provenance. Note every field you cannot fill from the sentence alone.

\item Find a question your retrieval system answers plausibly and wrongly. Identify which of the four consumption modes it actually needed.

\item Compute the intersection of two overlapping policy sets in your domain. Inspect the differences before the union.

\item Take a rule your agents are expected to follow and make it refuse an effect in a test.

\item List the decision-material fields of one document your agents read, and write the consistency checks that would catch a forged one.

\end{enumerate}

\BookNeedspace{220.1pt}

\section{Where this leads next}

The knowledge layer defines what the organization knows and what agents may do with it. Chapter 6 turns to the run itself: how context is assembled, how memory and workflow state differ, and how a single request gets exactly the knowledge it needs and nothing more.\par

\BookChapterCover{cover-images/chapter-06-build-context-without-losing-the-plot.png}

\BookChapter{06}{Build Context Without Losing the Plot}

\begin{bookcallout}[Where The Mortgage Case Stands]
As Harborlight's evidence runs grow, prompts mix conversation, workflow state, retrieved policy, summaries, and remembered preferences. The platform keeps these sources separate and records exactly which authorized projection reached each model call.
\end{bookcallout}

\begin{bookcallout}[The Design Choice]
Treat prompts as disposable authorized projections; keep canonical state, knowledge, artifacts, and memory in distinct stores.
\end{bookcallout}

\section{Why context needs an architecture of its own}

The prompt is the model's working view, not the system's memory. It may contain conversation, workflow state, retrieved evidence, summaries, and preferences, all with different authority and retention rules. This chapter shows how to assemble that view deliberately, how to keep memory from becoming an unreviewed shadow database, and why these distinctions matter when context is large, sensitive, stale, or later challenged in an audit. Chapter 3's AgentStepRequest carries an authorized ContextManifest; this chapter defines how it is built and recorded. Knowledge arrives through the Enterprise Knowledge Hub of Chapter 5.\par

\BookNeedspace{51.4pt}
\BookTableStart
\fontsize{7.2}{9}\selectfont
\begin{tabular}{@{}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)/5\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)/5\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)/5\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)/5\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-8\tabcolsep)/5\relax}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{State owner} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Trust boundary} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Scale unit} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Failure containment} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Core contracts} \\
Context Service; workflow remains canonical & authorized sources to model prompt & tenant · source · context budget & one context projection & ContextManifest · MemoryRecord · EvidenceRef \\
\end{tabular}
\BookTableEnd

\section{Five sources, one disposable projection}

These sources often appear together in a prompt, which makes it tempting to store them together. They have different jobs. Workflow state says what must happen next and must survive a restart; it is canonical in the durable run (Chapter 15). Conversation is the interaction history. Enterprise knowledge supplies governed facts and rules (Chapter 5). Working memory holds run-scoped hypotheses, and long-term memory holds approved reusable facts under their own retention policy. Context is only the temporary projection of these five for one step. Mixing them makes deletion, authorization, freshness, and recovery ambiguous—and turns prompt history into an accidental source of truth.\par

\BookTableStart
\fontsize{7.2}{9}\selectfont
\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Source} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Scope and examples} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{System of record} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Rules} \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Source} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Scope and examples} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{System of record} & \color{white}\bfseries \textbf{Rules} \\
\endhead
Workflow state & Current node, variables, child tasks, budgets, approvals & Durable run history (Chapter 15); context reads the RunStateProjection & Strong transition rules; versioned; queryable; replay-safe \\*
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Conversation & Recent messages, channel state, current clarification & Conversation service \slash{} document DB & Append-only turns; compact with source links; not authoritative business state \\*
Enterprise knowledge & Policies, contracts, code, tickets, records, catalogs & Source systems + governed search\slash{}vector\slash{}graph indexes, through the Hub (Chapter 5) & Source remains authoritative; resolved against the release the run pinned; ACL filter before retrieval; freshness and lineage \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
Working memory & Scratch notes, candidate plan, intermediate hypotheses & Run-scoped state or object artifacts & TTL; no automatic cross-run reuse; label confidence and provenance \\
Long-term memory & Approved preferences, reusable facts, learned procedures & Governed memory service \slash{} relational + search index & Explicit write policy, consent, provenance, TTL, ACL, correction\slash{}deletion \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

Artifacts, telemetry, audit, secrets, and caches live in the stores of Chapter 4 § Where each kind of state belongs. Artifacts produced by other agents or trust boundaries enter context only after adoption (Chapter 18; Chapter 19 shows the cross-boundary adoption check).\par

\section{How context is assembled}

Everything the model sees on a given step is produced by a pipeline, and the order of its stages is a security property rather than an implementation detail. The pipeline admits five kinds of source—pinned instructions and policy constraints, the run's state projection, a minimal conversation window, enterprise knowledge, and memory—and each passes every stage.\par

\BookDiagram{assets/diagrams/context-pipeline.pdf}{Figure 6-1 — The context build pipeline: authorization precedes retrieval, and bytes materialize last}{context-pipeline}

\BookTableStart
\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)/3\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)/3\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)/3\relax}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries Stage & \color{white}\bfseries What it does & \color{white}\bfseries What it prevents \\
\endfirsthead
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries Stage & \color{white}\bfseries What it does & \color{white}\bfseries What it prevents \\
\endhead
1 · Parse request & Extract intent, entities, and the declared purpose & Retrieval driven by raw user text \\*
\rowcolor{BookLight}
2 · Candidate sources & Choose candidate sources, including memory, which passes its own policy gate & Searching corpora the purpose does not justify \\*
3 · Authorize & Filter by tenant, subject, and resource ACL & The classic RAG leak: retrieving what the caller may not read \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
4 · Fetch pinned versions & Read immutable snapshots named in the RunManifest (Chapter 4) & A run that cannot be replayed because sources moved \\
5 · Classify and sanitize & Label each segment's kind and trust tier (see the hierarchy below), re-check the data label assigned at ingestion (Chapter 5), strip active content & Instructions embedded in retrieved documents \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
6 · Deduplicate & Collapse near-identical chunks & One loud document crowding out the rest of the evidence \\
7 · Rank & Order by relevance, freshness, source authority and effective dates (Chapter 5) & Similarity alone deciding what the model believes \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
8 · Allocate tokens & Allocate tokens within per-section caps fixed before retrieval & Silent truncation dropping the constraint that mattered \\
9 · Ordered references & Emit references, not content & Oversized payloads and unattributable claims \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
10 · ContextManifest & Record exactly what was assembled and why, and record its hash on the model span & An answer nobody can audit \\
\end{longtable}
\BookTableEnd

Two ordering decisions carry most of the safety. \textbf{Authorization is stage three, before any content is fetched} — filtering results after retrieval means the unauthorized bytes already existed in a process that could log or cache them. And \textbf{only the final boundary materializes bytes} into the model request; everything upstream moves references, which is what keeps the manifest an accurate record of what the model actually saw.\par

\subsection{The trust hierarchy of instructions}

The model receives text from several origins in one window, and it has no inherent way to rank them. The platform must, because the ranking is what stops a document from behaving like an instruction.\par

\BookNeedspace{106.3pt}
\BookTableStart
\fontsize{7.2}{9}\selectfont
\begin{tabular}{@{}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}Y{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)/4\relax}@{}}
\rowcolor{BookNavy}
\color{white}\bfseries Tier & \color{white}\bfseries Origin & \color{white}\bfseries Trust & \color{white}\bfseries Can it direct behavior? \\
1 & System and application instructions & Release artifact, code-reviewed & Yes — this is the definition of the agent \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
2 & Skill instructions (Chapter 8) & Pinned, versioned capability artifact & Yes, within the capability's declared scope \\
3 & User request & Authenticated intent, bounded by the caller's authority & It states a goal; it cannot grant itself authority \\
\rowcolor{BookLight}
4 & Retrieved documents and tool output & Untrusted evidence, whatever its source & \textbf{No.} It is data to reason over, never direction \\
\end{tabular}
\BookTableEnd

Workflow-state projections, conversation history, and memories are tier-four data with different authority as facts: run state is canonical, conversation is a record of what was said, and memory is advisory. None of them can direct behavior. Each segment therefore carries one \lstinline[style=bookinline]!kind!: \lstinline[style=bookinline]!system_instruction! (tier 1), \lstinline[style=bookinline]!skill_instruction! (tier 2), \lstinline[style=bookinline]!user_request! (tier 3), or one of \lstinline[style=bookinline]!run_state!, \lstinline[style=bookinline]!conversation!, \lstinline[style=bookinline]!enterprise_data!, \lstinline[style=bookinline]!tool_result!, and \lstinline[style=bookinline]!memory! (tier 4).\par

Content cannot promote itself between tiers. A retrieved document that contains “ignore previous instructions” is a tier-four artifact containing a string; it does not become tier one by asserting that it should. This is why tool eligibility is decided by policy before the step runs and never by what the model has just read — the one rule that makes indirect prompt injection a containable problem rather than an open one.\par

Retrieval can also happen inside a step, through a read-only tool behind the Tool Gateway (Chapter 3); it calls the same Hub, and its results enter the next model call as tier-four segments in a new manifest revision.\par

\section{Memory is not a bag of embeddings}

One source feeding this pipeline behaves differently from the rest. Retrieval reads; memory \emph{writes}, and a write that outlives the run is a protected data effect rather than a caching convenience. Creating long-term memory therefore carries the same obligations as any other durable write: an owner, a scope, provenance, a confidence, the policy basis that permitted it, a TTL, and a correction and deletion path. The write validates contradiction and sensitivity before it lands, and derived guesses default to run-scoped working memory, not long-term memory. And when a remembered summary disagrees with canonical business state, canonical state wins — always, and without the model being asked to adjudicate. Chapter 17 catalogs ungoverned memory writes as AP-13 and summary-as-state as AP-02.\par

\subsection{How the pattern works}

Working memory is run-scoped; long-term memory carries the obligations above.\par

\begin{enumerate}

\item Identify a candidate reusable fact.

\item Validate evidence, subject, purpose, consent or policy basis, and sensitivity.

\item Write a versioned memory record with TTL and provenance.

\item Retrieve only after authorization and trust labeling.

\item Correct, supersede, expire, or delete through propagation workflows.

\end{enumerate}

\BookTableStart
\small

\subsection{Where it can fail—and how to contain it}
